    "type": "skill_listing",
    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"SWI-Prolog backend.\\n\\nCalls the `swipl` binary via subprocess. Input is written as a temp .pl file.\\nQueries are embedded as directives: :- (Query -> halt(0) ; halt(1)).\\n\\nExit codes:\\n  0 — query succeeded (proved)\\n  1 — query failed (not proved)\\n  2 — fallback halt (syntax error / directive didn't execute)\\n\\nDependencies: swipl (SWI-Prolog) in PATH.\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport os\\nimport shutil\\nimport subprocess\\nimport tempfile\\nimport time\\n\\nfrom .base import FOLSolver, SolverResult\\n\\n\\nclass PrologSolver(FOLSolver):\\n\\n    def __init__(self, binary: str = \\\"swipl\\\"):\\n        self._binary = binary\\n\\n    def is_available(self) -> bool:\\n        return shutil.which(self._binary) is not None\\n\\n    def prove(self, premises: list[str], conclusion: str, timeout: int = 5) -> SolverResult:\\n        input_text = self._build_input(premises, conclusion)\\n        return self._run(input_text, timeout)\\n\\n    def check_consistency(self, formulas: list[str], timeout: int = 5) -> SolverResult:\\n        body = \\\"\\\\n\\\".join(self._ensure_dot(f) for f in formulas)\\n        body += \\\"\\\\n:- halt(0).\\\\n\\\"\\n        result = self._run(body, timeout)\\n        return SolverResult(\\n            success=result.success,\\n            proved=result.success and result.error is None,\\n            time_seconds=result.time_seconds,\\n            raw_output=result.raw_output,\\n            error=result.error,\\n        )\\n\\n    @staticmethod\\n    def _ensure_dot(clause: str) -> str:\\n        clause = clause.strip()\\n        if clause and not clause.endswith('.'):\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.52}}"}

{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"Prolog STA reward: execution + ISR for Prolog formalizations.\\n\\nMirrors src/sta/reward.py but for Prolog logic type:\\n- Parses Prolog clauses + query from model output\\n- Uses PrologSolver (swipl) instead of Prover9\\n- Ablation: remove one clause at a time, check if query breaks\\n- ISR = necessary_clauses / total_clauses\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nfrom __future__ import annotations\\n\\nimport re\\nimport time\\nfrom typing import Optional\\n\\nfrom ..solvers.base import FOLSolver, SolverResult\\nfrom .ablation import AblationResult\\nfrom .components import FOLComponent\\n\\n\\ndef parse_prolog_formalization(text: str) -> tuple[list[str], str]:\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Extract Prolog clauses and query from model output.\\n\\n    Supported formats:\\n\\n    1. Inline query:\\n        parent(tom, bob).\\n        grandparent(X,Y) :- parent(X,Z), parent(Z,Y).\\n        ?- grandparent(tom, ann).\\n\\n    2. Labeled sections:\\n        Facts/Rules:\\n        parent(tom, bob).\\n        Query: grandparent(tom, ann)\\n\\n    Returns (clauses, query). Query has no '?-' prefix or trailing '.'.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    lines = text.strip().splitlines()\\n    query: Optional[str] = None\\n    clauses: list[str] = []\\n\\n    for raw_line in lines:\\n        line = raw_line.strip()\\n        if not line or line.startswith('%'):\\n            continue\\n\\n        if line.startswith('?-'):\\n            query = line[2:].strip().rstrip('.')\\n            continue\\n\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.51}}"}

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Key findings so far:

1. **Prolog configs**: Both `grpo_prolog_exec_only.yaml` and `grpo_prolog_exec_sta.yaml` exist and look reasonable.
   - BUT: `num_steps: 100` — plan006 requires 500 steps. This needs to be overridden at launch.
   - `data_path: data/train_500_seed42.jsonl` — this is FOL data, not Prolog data. This is a critical issue!
   - `logic_type: prolog` and `solver: prolog` are set correctly.

2. **SWI-Prolog**: My initial search in /root/ came up empty, but swipl is typically installed in /usr/bin/ or /usr/local/bin/, so I need to check those standard locations. The ROADMAP claims it's already installed, but I haven't confirmed it yet.

3. **Prolog solver code**: The `PrologSolver` class checks if swipl is available in the PATH using `shutil.which()`, so the binary needs to be accessible from there.

4. **Prolog reward code**: Still examining this component...

5. **Data mismatch**: All the training data files are in FOL format (proofwriter_fol_*.jsonl), but the configs are pointing to FOL data while the logic type is set to Prolog. This raises a key question — is the design supposed to use FOL data with Prolog logic, or do I need Prolog-specific training data?

I need to verify whether swipl is actually installed system-wide and clarify whether the data format aligns with what the Prolog setup expects.
```

formalization += \\\"\\\\n\\\"\\n            formalization += f\\\"?- {item.get('prolog_query', '')}.\\\\n\\\"\\n        elif \\\"formalization\\\" in item:\\n            formalization = item[\\\"formalization\\\"]\\n        else:\\n            print(f\\\"[{i}] SKIP: no formalization\\\")\\n            continue\\n\\n        result = reward_fn(item, formalization, solver, timeout=5)\\n parser.add_argument(\\\"--fast\\\", action=\\\"store_true\\\", help=\\\"Use fast O(1) ISR\\\")\\n    parser.add_argument(\\\"--timeout\\\", type=int, default=5)\\n    args = parser.parse_args()\\n\\n    solver = get_solver(\\\"prolog\\\")\\n    print(f\\\"swipl available: {solver.is_available()}\\\")\\n\\n    if args.test:\\n        ok = run_tests(solver, use_fast=args.fast)\\n        sys.exit(0 if    - ✅ `configs/grpo_prolog_exec_only.yaml` — but `num_steps: 100` (plan006 needs 500)
   - ✅ `configs/grpo_prolog_exec_sta.yaml` — but `num_steps: 100` (plan006 needs 500)

4. **Data**: ⚠️ Critical issue! The training data `train_500_seed42.jsonl` contains FOL data with `fol_premises`, which is incompatible with the Prolog setup that expects different input formats.

Actually, wait — I'm reconsidering this. The GRPO training loop has the model generate Prolog code from the natural language context and question, so the training data just needs to provide the NL input and the model learns to formalize it in Prolog. The FOL fields in the data aren't necessarily a blocker since the model isn't directly using them.

But I'm concerned about whether those FOL fields might be getting fed into the prompt somehow, which would confuse a Prolog-focused training run. I need to look at the actual training script to see how it consumes the data and constructs prompts. I should also verify whether the readiness check actually ran the Prolog validation and check the GPU status.

若 ISR > random ≈ exec-only → ISR signal 是关键; 若 ISR ≈ random → ISR 无结构价值。 ← 基于 plan013-random-reward-s42-r4\\n  [eval_sta_plan017_additive_random_s42](/api/workspace-files/projects/solver-trace-ablation/artifacts/plan017-additive-random-s42/data/eval_sta_plan017_additive_random_s42.txt)\\n- **plan001-exec-only-s44** (negative): Exec-only seed=44 第3 seed: 解决 s42(93% eval PR) vs s43(13% eval PR) 的跨 seed 不稳定问题，3-seed mean±std 是论文必需。\\n  [eval_sta_plan001_exec_only_s44](/api/workspace-files/projects/solver-trace-ablation/artifacts/plan001-exec-only-s44/data/eval_sta_plan001_exec_only_s44.json)\\n- **plan014-downstream-nli-s42** (negative): Downstream NLI validation: 高ISR formalization 应在独立推理任务上表现更好(entailment accuracy)，Spearman ρ(ISR, accuracy) > 0.1 验证 ISR 作为 quality proxy 的实用价值。同时用 Z3 cross-solver agreement 验证 formalization 鲁棒性。\\n\\n## 趋势\\n成功 6 / 负向 9 / 失败 14 / 共 29\\n\\n## 下一步\\n（无活跃 idea，请通过 ideas.md 添加）\\n\\n## 资源状态\\nwestd-39279: 无 GPU 信息\\n\\n## 告警\\n- sta-phase1-exec-only: 训练失败 — unknown\\n- sta-phase1-exec-sta: 训练失败 — unknown\\n- sta-phase1-exec-only-r2: 训练失败 — unknown\\n- sta-phase1-exec-sta-r2: 训练失败 — unknown\\n- sta-phase1-exec-only-r3: 训练失败 — unknown\\n- sta-phase1-exec-sta-r3: 训练失败 — unknown\\n- plan001-exec-only-s42: 训练失败 — unknown\\n- plan001-exec-only-s42-r3: 训练失败 — unknown\\n- plan001-exec-only-s43: 训练失败 — unknown\\n- plan013-random-reward-s42: 训练失败 — unknown\\n- plan013-random-reward-s42-r2: 训练失败 — unknown\\n- plan001-exec-only-s43-r2: 训练失败 — unknown\\n- plan013-random-reward-s42-r3: 训练失败 — unknown\\n- plan001-exec-only-s43-r3: 训练失败 — unknown\\n\", \"total_chars\": 2169, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have a complete picture. Let me also check if the config properly sets the `solver` parameter to `prolog`, since the factory will be dispatching based on it.

Looking at the configs:
- `grpo_prolog_exec_only.yaml`: has `solver: prolog` — this will cause `get_solver("prolog")` to instantiate `PrologSolver`, which checks `shutil.which("swipl")`. Since swipl is at `/usr/bin/swipl`, this should work.

The code flow for Prolog GRPO:
1. Config sets `logic_type: prolog` and `solver: prolog`


2. `main()` reads the logic type and builds the Prolog dataset with the appropriate system prompt
3. The solver factory instantiates `PrologSolver` after verifying swipl is available
4. The reward function uses Prolog-specific scoring with either standard or fast ablation methods
5. The model generates Prolog code from natural language, which gets executed and evaluated through ISR computation

I need to check whether end-to-end validation was actually performed, since I don't see evidence of the test suite being run.

The training data structure has FOL-specific fields that aren't directly used — the dataset builder only pulls from the `context` and `question` fields, leaving `prolog_premises` and `prolog_query` empty. This is fine since the reward function evaluates based on execution behavior rather than ground truth comparison.

There's also a configuration issue where both Prolog setups specify 100 steps instead of the 500 needed for plan006, which would need to be corrected through command-line overrides.

I need to verify whether `PROLOG_USER_TEMPLATE` is actually defined in the codebase — I found the system prompt but haven't located the user template yet.
```

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

````text
Now I have a thorough understanding. Let me also check the argument parsing carefully. I notice a potential bug in the config merge logic:

```python
if args.config:
    cfg = load_yaml_config(args.config)
    for k, v in cfg.items():
        if hasattr(args, k):
            current = getattr(args, k)
            if current is None or (isinstance(v, bool) and v and not current):
                setattr(args, k, v)
        else:
            setattr(args, k, v)

# Apply defaults for optional args after config merge
if args.solver is None:
    args.solver = "prover9"
if args.logic_type is None:
    args.logic_type = "fol"
```

The config merge logic applies values only when the current argument is None, so with the config having `solver: prolog` and `logic_type: prolog`, those values will override the None defaults. After the merge, both get set to "prolog", which looks correct. But wait—

The config merge condition is more nuanced than I initially thought. It only applies the config value if the CLI argument is None or if it's a boolean that's true but the current value is falsy. So for the training setup, the config file `grpo_prolog_exec_only.yaml` sets `solver: prolog`, `logic_type: prolog`, and `num_steps: 100`, but I need to pass `--num_steps 500` on the command line to override that 100. The key issue is understanding exactly when the config values take precedence—it's only when the CLI argument hasn't been explicitly set. The reward code, factory support, training script with proper Prolog integration, config files, test script, and data compatibility all check out—everything needed for the Prolog solver is in place.

However, I haven't actually run the validation tests to confirm the pipeline works end-to-end. The configs default to 100 steps instead of the 500 specified in plan006, though CLI overrides can handle this. I also need to verify that `eval_sta.py` supports Prolog evaluation, since that's critical for post-training assessment.

<details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
`eval_sta.py` fully supports Prolog via `--logic_type prolog`. It imports `prolog_sta_reward` and has a `LOGIC_CONFIG["prolog"]` section. ✅

Now let me compile my full assessment and post to director.

## Complete Readiness Assessment:

### ✅ Ready components:
1. SWI-Prolog: `/usr/bin/swipl` installed
2. PrologSolver: `src/solvers/prolog.py` + factory registration
3. Prolog STA reward: `src/sta/prolog_reward.py` (ablation-based ISR)


4. Training script: `train_grpo.py` with complete Prolog path (PROLOG_SYSTEM_PROMPT, build_prolog_dataset, make_reward_fn dispatch)
5. Evaluation script: `eval_sta.py` supports `--logic_type prolog`
6. Configs: `grpo_prolog_exec_only.yaml` + `grpo_prolog_exec_sta.yaml`
7. Test script: `scripts/sta_prolog.py` with 5 test cases
8. Data compatibility: FOL data `train_500_seed42.jsonl` is compatible (uses `context`/`question` fields, model generates)

Now I'm noticing some gaps: there's no evidence the smoke test was actually run to validate the swipl integration and ISR calculation work end-to-end. The Prolog configs both default to 100 steps when Plan006 requires 500, and I'll need to handle multiple seeds (42, 43, 44) for the three D024 runs rather than relying on the hardcoded seed value.

The biggest risk is skipping that smoke test—if there's a parsing issue or swipl timeout problem, plan006 could fail immediately and waste a GPU slot. I should post these findings to the director.
```

  "text": "## 审查报告 — worker_prep_plan006-prolog-readiness\n\n**结论**: WARNING\n\n### 已验证就绪组件\n- ✅ SWI-Prolog: `/usr/bin/swipl` 已安装\n- ✅ PrologSolver (`src/solvers/prolog.py`) + factory 注册 (`get_solver(\"prolog\")`)\n- ✅ Prolog STA reward (`src/sta/prolog_reward.py`): ablation-based ISR，parse_prolog_formalization 支持两种格式\n- ✅ train_grpo.py: PROLOG_SYSTEM_PROMPT + PROLOG_USER_TEMPLATE + build_prolog_dataset() + make_reward_fn dispatch（logic_type=\"prolog\" 分支完整）\n- ✅ eval_sta.py: `--logic_type prolog` 支持（LOGIC_CONFIG[\"prolog\"] 映射到 prolog_sta_reward）\n- ✅ 配置文件: `grpo_prolog_exec_only.yaml` 和 `grpo_prolog_exec_sta.yaml`，`solver: prolog` + `logic_type: prolog` 正确设置\n- ✅ 数据兼容性: `train_500_seed42.jsonl` 的 `context`/`question` 字段被 `build_prolog_dataset()` 使用，FOL-specific 字段（fol_premises 等）不影响 Prolog 训练路径\n- ✅ 测试脚本: `scripts/sta_prolog.py` 有 5 个内置 test cases\n\n### 发现\n\n- [severity: warning] **缺少端到端 smoke test 证据**。服务器上找不到 `sta_prolog.py --test` 的执行日志或输出。Worker 创建/检查了代码和配置，但没有证据表明实际验证过 swipl 集成 → Prolog 解析 → ISR 计算 → reward 返回这条完整链路。如果 swipl 的超时处理、Prolog 语法解析或 reward 函数有 bug，plan006 可能启动后立即失败，浪费 GPU 时间。**建议：启动 plan006 训练前，先在服务器执行 `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES= python3 scripts/sta_prolog.py --test`（不占 GPU），验证 5/5 pass。**\n\n- [severity: info] 两个 Prolog 配置默认 `num_steps: 100`（plan006 需要 500）。可通过 CLI `--num_steps 500` 覆盖，但建议更新配置默认值避免误启动。\n\n- [severity: info] Plan006 按 D024 需要 3 seeds (42/43/44)。配置里 `seed: 42` 硬编码，其余两个 seed 需 CLI 覆盖 `--seed 43/44`。\n\n### GPU 资源合规\n- plan002-b01-s42 在 cuda:0 RUNNING ✅\n- plan016-extended-1000-s42 在 cuda:1 RUNNING ✅\n- plan006 需等当前训练完成后启动（ROADMAP 已注明）",
